TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 5.98437
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (one per PE, 256 total, lines 4666-4718): a <- {1'b0, a} (the seq_mac port a, i.e. the PE's in_a, 8 bits zero-extended to 9); b <- {1'b0, b} (the seq_mac port b, i.e. the PE's in_b[DWIDTH-1:0], line 4455); clk <- clk; reset <- the existing, currently unused seq_mac port reset, which every PE drives with effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110, instances 4112-4369, 4455/4551/4648); out[15:0] -> the new soft register out_temp (same name, same 16-bit width, same readers); out[17:16]` unused.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, line 4110) is asserted for at least one clock edge at power-up before any matmul data enters the array, and is not asserted again while any PE accumulator or in-flight product is nonzero (i.e. a reset marks the start of an accumulation sequence, not a point inside one). Support: seq_mac never clears its own registers (lines 4683-4701: a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp have no reset), so effective_rst is the only clear available to the block, while the block contract requires one; pe_reset is a self-clearing software bit written through the APB register REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR (lines 4852, 4868, 4883) and is the design's "put the PE array in a known state" control, matching PE register behaviour at lines 4457-4466; whenever top-level reset is high the operands entering the array are already forced to 0 (a_mem_access_counter cleared, line 2597, so a_data_valid=0 and a*_data=0, lines 2609-2647; the delay pipeline cleared, line 2772), and the PE out_a/out_b registers are cleared by effective_rst (line 4458), so zeroing the block's operand registers at those edges removes only zero-valued terms. Under this assumption the rewrite is cycle-exact; without it the rewrite would differ from the original only in that the original keeps accumulating across a pe_reset (the original hardware never clears its accumulators).

Converted region — qmult.$mul$...:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances

  • What it is: qmult (line 4725) is an unsigned 8x8 multiply (input [DWIDTH-1:0] operands, so Verilog multiplies unsigned; no sign handling and no correction logic is needed), its 16-bit product is registered in mul_out_temp_reg (line 4692) and added by qadd (line 4735) into out_temp (line 4700), which wraps at 16 bits. This is exactly acc += a*b`.
  • Fit to the block: operands 8 bits zero-extend to the 9-bit ports (a,b <= 255, product <= 65025 < 2^16). The block's running sum wraps mod 2^18; its low 16 bits are the sum mod 2^16, bit-identical to the original 16-bit qadd accumulator, because truncation of a modular sum to 16 bits is the same as summing mod 2^16. out_temp[17:16] is discarded. All terms kept, no term computed outside, no constant ties.
  • Visibility: a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult, mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd, so all three may disappear inside the block. out_temp is read by the downcast at lines 4704-4716 (all 16 bits: out_temp[15], out_temp[14:7], out_temp[6:0]), so it is kept as a real soft register outside the block, not hidden.

Edit for the editor (entirely inside module seq_mac, lines 4666-4718; all 256 instances change at once because every PE instantiates this one module — processing_element 4455, _top_edge 4551, _bottom_edge 4648):

  1. Delete the a_flopped/b_flopped declarations and their always block (4677-4678, 4683-4686), the qmult instance (4689), mul_out_temp_reg and its always block (4681, 4691-4693), the qadd instance (4697), and the now-unused wires mul_out, add_out, mul_out_temp. Leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file (they become uninstantiated).
  2. Add wire [17:0] mac_out; and instantiate, connecting every input port by name:
    mac_int_9x9 u_mac (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(mac_out));
  3. Keep reg [2*DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp;` and drive it from the block, with a synchronous clear on the same reset so no X survives the first clear:
    always @(posedge clk) begin if (reset) out_temp <= 0; else out_temp <= mac_out[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; end`
  4. Leave the downcast assign out = ... (4704-4716) exactly as it is.
    Nothing outside seq_mac changes: no new port, no new net at the PE or array level (the reset port already arrives at every instance).

Register stages, before and after (converted region)

  • Before: operands at the seq_mac inputs -> stage 1 a_flopped/b_flopped -> stage 2 mul_out_temp_reg -> stage 3 out_temp. Three stages; a product from operands sampled at edge k lands in out_temp at edge k+2.
  • After: operands -> stage 1 the block's operand registers -> stage 2 the block's out running sum -> stage 3 the new soft out_temp. Three stages, same latency: out_temp after edge n = sum of products of operands sampled at edges <= n-2, identical to the original every cycle. This is why the block's 2-cycle latency is absorbed with one soft register after the block rather than before it; the alternative (keeping a_flopped/b_flopped and using out directly as out_temp) has the same register and area count but pushes the block's output register into the 4-LUT path that ends at output_logic's c_data_out flops — the baseline's slowest stage — exchanging a soft-to-soft hop for a "block into soft logic" hop (median 0.96, up to 1.90 ns) on the one path that must not grow. Putting the register on the block's output leaves that stage structurally untouched (soft flip-flop -> same LUTs -> same flip-flop).

Candidates left, with reasons

  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 regions, tanh slope multiply, line 5479): left. (a) Each is a single per-cycle product truncated to 8 bits, not a running sum; an always-accumulating block cannot emit a standalone product — clearing it on alternate edges does not help, because the same clear also zeroes the operand registers, so the first edge after a clear adds 0*0 and out stays 0. (b) slope_applied_data_internal is written under a clock enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress, line 5474) and holds its value whenever activation_type==1'b0 (the ReLU branch at 5484 does not assign it); the block has no enable and no hold. (c) The addend in the mult_add is data_intercept_delayed (line 5482), a LUT-table value in a later register stage, not another product, so it cannot be a term of the block's own running sum.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (sop, sum_in=top) and $380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (multiply) (16 regions, line 5067): left, same core reason as above. Each is one 8-bit-truncated product per cycle written into variance_applied_data under the enable in_data_available || norm_in_progress with clears on both the reset and the idle branch (lines 5047, 5056, 5094-5101); there is no running sum of products to map onto acc += a*b, and the block offers no enable, no load and no way to present a bare product.

Blocks, tiles and area

  • The rewrite instantiates 256 blocks (one per seq_mac), all on clk and all on the one reset effective_rst, so four may share a tile: 64 tiles for the whole PE array.
  • The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in norm, 16 in activation) stay as they are. Charging them the baseline's observed loose density (56 of 88 tiles hold four each; the other 64 multiplies sit two to a tile), they keep 16 tiles: 64 + 16 = 80 tiles of the device's 132, down 8 from 88. If those 32 pack four to a tile the total is 72; 80 is the conservative figure. The count of DSP-resident operations does not rise (256 multiplies before, 256 blocks after), so the tile count cannot exceed the baseline's 88.
  • Soft logic removed: per PE, mul_out_temp_reg (16 flip-flops), the old out_temp (16 flip-flops) and the 16-bit qadd carry chain, minus the 16 flip-flops added back for the new out_temp, and a_flopped/b_flopped (16 flip-flops) — net 32 flip-flops and 16 adder bits per PE, i.e. about 8192 flip-flops and 4096 adder bits across the array. At roughly two flip-flops per BLE and ten BLEs per cluster that is on the order of 400 clusters (~1.1e7 MWTA), plus 8 tiles (2.03e6 MWTA): used logic area should fall from 5.69025e7 to roughly 4.3e7-4.8e7. Wirelength should fall with it (the per-PE 16-bit multiply-to-register, register-to-adder, adder-to-register and accumulator feedback nets all go away, replaced by one operand pair into the tile and one 16-bit tile-to-register net), expect roughly 2.7e5-3.0e5. With delay unchanged the objective lands near 7e13-8e13 against the baseline 1.10461e14.

Timing

  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. A block is never in series with another block — each PE's accumulator is independent and the array's inter-PE connections run through out_a/out_b registers (lines 4457-4466). That stage is the block's internal 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's slowest stage of 5.98437 ns.
  • New stages created: (1) source register (the upstream PE's out_a/out_b flop, or the systolic_data_setup delay flop) straight into the block's input register, no logic between: 0.060 + wire register-to-first-block 1.419-2.461 (median 1.732) + 0.019 = 1.50-2.54 ns; (2) the block's 2.219 ns; (3) the block's output register into the new soft out_temp flop, no logic between: 0.060 + wire last-block-to-register 0.793-1.975 (median 1.416) + flip-flop setup = about 0.9-2.1 ns. All three are far below 5.98437 ns even at the top of the sampled wire ranges.
  • Soft logic that leaves a stage: a_flopped/b_flopped and mul_out_temp_reg leave the stage "register -> hard mult_9x9_fixed_pt -> flip-flop" (the baseline's second-slowest stage, 5.95 ns: wire 1.73 in, 1.98 out) — that stage is deleted outright and replaced by stages (1)-(3) above. The 16-bit qadd carry chain and its feedback leave the stage mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp. Neither of these is the slowest stage.
  • The slowest stage, the one in output_logic (register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable, 5.98437 ns, with 2.64 ns of wire into the last LUT), is not touched: its source flip-flop, its LUTs and its destination flip-flop are unchanged, and the one path that feeds it from the converted region (out_temp -> downcast at 4704-4716 -> the c_data_out* capture mux at 2327-2346) still starts at a soft flip-flop with the identical downcast logic behind it. So the clock period stays at 5.98437 ns; the gain is area and wirelength, with a possible secondary delay gain from the 8 freed tiles and ~400 freed clusters shortening that 2.64 ns wire. Nothing in the rewrite makes another stage slower than it.

ACCEPT

seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666-4708) is the only changed region, matching the plan's single region and its named deletions/additions; qmult/qadd remain as uninstantiated definitions (4710, 4720) as the plan allowed, and no other reader of them exists.

  • Ports: mac_int_9x9 u_mac_hard (4680) connects clk, reset, a, b by name — all four inputs; operands are 8-bit (DWIDTH = 8, line 74) zero-extended with {1'b0, a} / {1'b0, b}, and reset is the already-present seq_mac port driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (4110) at every PE (4455/4551/4648). Instance name differs from the plan's u_mac only to avoid colliding with the PE-level name; no behavioral effect.
  • Terms/arithmetic: the original qmult is an unsigned 8×8 product, exact in 16 bits (≤ 65025), accumulated by the 16-bit qadd mod 2^16. The block's running sum mod 2^18 truncated to mac_out[15:0] (4689) is the same value bit-for-bit. No term dropped, nothing computed outside, no constant ties.
  • Registers/latency: original stages a_flopped/b_flopped → mul_out_temp_reg → out_temp (3 stages, product of operands sampled at edge k lands in out_temp after edge k+2). After: block operand register → block out (contract: in out after edge k+1) → new soft out_temp at edge k+2. out_temp after edge n = Σ products of operands sampled at edges ≤ n-2 in both versions.
  • Visibility: a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp_reg had no readers outside the deleted logic; out_temp is read by the downcast at 4694-4706 and is kept as a real register outside the block, unchanged in name and 16-bit width, so the out downcast and its downstream readers are untouched.
  • Reset/power-up: out_temp gets a synchronous clear on the same reset (4685-4687), so no X survives the first clear and nothing relies on an initial value. The original seq_mac registers had no clear, so clearing on effective_rst is correct only under the plan's stated ASSUMES: (reset precedes any data and never lands inside an accumulation with nonzero accumulator or in-flight products), which cites 4683-4701 of the original, 4110, 4457-4466, 2597, 2609-2647, 2772 — consistent with the code read here.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.98437 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 324384, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.10461e+14. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT. The next slowest stage, in a multiply stage (the end register's module not established), is one hard multiply on a DSP tile (mult_9x9_fixed_pt), from a register straight into the multiply and from the multiply straight into a flip-flop; wire 1.73 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.98 from the multiply into the flip-flop, 5.95 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds. Registers the rewrite adds have no initial value to rely on either.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The rewrite is verified under that assumption.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A DSP tile holds four blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

The down-cast result is then registered into the module's 8-bit output out (new 8 flip-flops per PE), which replaces the 16-bit out_temp` register of the kept candidate.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, line 4110) is asserted at least once before the first operands are applied, and is asserted only at points where the PE accumulators are meant to restart from zero. Support: the original out_temp (lines 4699-4701) has no reset path at all, so the block's mandatory synchronous clear is the only way to reach the "every register of the original starts at 0" state required for a power-up-unknown block; pe_reset exists exactly to clear the PEs — it is a self-clearing config bit, written by software at REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR bit 15 (line 4883) and cleared again in IDLE (line 4868), itself reset to 0 at line 4852. This is the same assumption the kept candidate was built and measured under; the rewrite below is cycle-for-cycle identical to the kept candidate, so it is verified under exactly the same condition.

PLAN:

What changes versus the kept candidate. The kept candidate is right about which region to convert; its one weakness is where it puts the register that restores the third register stage. It keeps a 16-bit soft register (out_temp, 16 flip-flops x 256 PEs = 4096 flip-flops) between the block's out and the down-cast, so the design still contains the full baseline stage out_temp -> down-cast (3 LUT levels) -> output_logic mux -> c_data_out — the stage the baseline reports as its slowest (5.98 ns, "a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT"; the 2.64 ns wire is the haul from a PE in the array to output_logic). This revision keeps everything else the same and moves that register to the far side of the down-cast: the down-cast is computed combinationally from the block's out, and only its 8-bit result is registered. Same values on every cycle, 8 flip-flops per PE instead of 16 (-2048 flip-flops overall), and the baseline's longest stage is cut into two roughly equal halves.

The edit (all of it inside module seq_mac, lines 4666-4718; no other module changes). Replace the body of seq_mac with:

  • output reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out; (the port becomes a register; it was a wire driven by the assign` at 4704).
  • Delete out_temp, mul_out, add_out, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp, mul_out_temp_reg, the two always blocks at 4683-4686 and 4691-4693, the qmult instance (4689), the qadd instance (4697), the always block at 4699-4701, and the assign out = ... at 4704-4716.
  • Instantiate mac_int_9x9 u_mac_hard(.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(mac_out)); with wire [17:0] mac_out;.
  • Add wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] sum_next = mac_out[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; — the low 16 bits of the block's running sum are exactly what the old 16-bit qadd accumulator held (both are the running sum taken modulo 2^16; the block wraps at 18 bits, so its low 16 bits wrap at 2^16 as well). Bits mac_out[17:16] are dropped, as the original truncated the same way.
  • Add a combinational wire [DWIDTH-1:0] downcast_next that is the expression of lines 4704-4716 verbatim with out_temp replaced by sum_next (sign bit sum_next[15] passed through; |sum_next[14:7] as the overflow test; saturate to all-ones / all-zeros or pass sum_next[6:0]`).
  • Add always @(posedge clk) begin if (reset) out <= 0; else out <= downcast_next; end.

out is the only thing the PE exposes from the mac (assign out_c = out_mac; at 4453/4549/4646), so nothing else reads out_temp or the product; no value moved inside a block is needed anywhere else, and no recomputation outside is required.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same. Write a(k), b(k) for the port values sampled at edge k and D() for the down-cast function. Original (lines 4683-4701): out_temp(n) = sum over j <= n-2 of a(j)b(j) mod 2^16, and out_c(n) = D(out_temp(n)). Block: operands sampled at edge k are added into out at edge k+1, so mac_out(n) = sum over j <= n-1 of a(j)b(j) mod 2^18; hence mac_out(n-1)[15:0] = out_temp(n). The new register gives out(n) = D(mac_out(n-1)[15:0]) = D(out_temp(n)) = out_c(n). At a reset edge the register is forced to 0, and D(0) = {0, 7'b0} = 0, so the reset branch agrees with the cleared accumulator; the block's own clear flushes its operand registers and its sum at the same edge, so after a reset at edge k both the kept candidate and this version produce the first non-zero result at edge k+3 from the operands sampled at edge k+1. This version is therefore identical, cycle by cycle, to the kept candidate that was already built and measured, and identical to the unmodified design under the ASSUMES line above.

Register stages, before and after (converted region). Before: in_a/in_b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2) -> qadd with out_temp -> out_temp (stage 3) -> combinational down-cast -> out_c. After: in_a/in_b -> block operand register (stage 1) -> multiply -> block out running-sum register (stage 2) -> combinational down-cast -> new 8-bit out register (stage 3) -> out_c. Three register stages before and after, so out_c carries the same value on the same cycle; no latency changes anywhere in the systolic array, and out_a/out_b (lines 4650-4659 etc.) are untouched.

Blocks and tiles. 256 blocks, one per seq_mac, all on the one net effective_rst and the one clock clk, so any four may share a tile. The block set, their port connections and their reset net are identical to the kept candidate, which packed into 116 DSP tiles at seed 1 (64 would be the four-to-a-tile ideal; the packer did not reach it, and nothing in this revision changes the block set, so 116 is the honest number to state rather than the ideal). The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in activation, 16 in norm) stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode and are included in that 116. Against the baseline's 88 tiles this is +28 tiles (+7.1e6 area units); it is paid for by the soft logic removed below and by the 16% shorter period and 14% shorter wirelength already measured.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block's output reaches another block: every out goes through soft logic into a register. The single-block stage is operand register -> multiply -> out register = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the slowest stage.

What soft logic leaves which stage, and which stage is slowest. Per PE, leaving the design: a_flopped + b_flopped (16 flip-flops) and mul_out_temp_reg (16 flip-flops) become the block's two internal register ranks; the 16-bit qadd (16 adder cells) becomes the block's running sum; out_temp (16 flip-flops) is replaced by an 8-bit register. Net per PE against the baseline: -40 flip-flops and -16 soft adder cells; across 256 PEs, -10240 flip-flops and -4096 adder cells. Against the kept candidate specifically, -2048 flip-flops. The stages affected:

  • The accumulate stage mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp disappears entirely (it was not the slowest).
  • The baseline's slowest stage, out_temp -> 3 down-cast LUT levels -> output_logic mux -> c_data_out (5.98 ns in the baseline, with a 2.64 ns wire into the last LUT), is split in two by the new register: (i) block out -> down-cast -> new register = 0.060 + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic) + ~2 x 0.861 (soft levels) + 0.153 + 0.019 ~ 2.9 ns, and (ii) new register -> 2.64 ns wire -> output_logic LUT -> c_data_out flip-flop ~ 2.9 ns. Both are far below the period, and this also removes the risk that would come from driving the long PE-to-output_logic haul straight out of a DSP output.
  • The slowest stage left is one this rewrite does not touch: the a-data soft path measured at seed 1 on the kept candidate, a register through four LUTs (new_n56609_1, new_n56607, new_n56604_1, $and~1165^Y~4) into the block's a input register, 5.037 ns, of which 2.85 ns is wire inside the LUT chain and 1.417 ns is the wire into the block. It ends at a block input register (setup 0.019), it contains no block and no arithmetic of any candidate region, and it bounds the period from below at this seed — hence STAGE_NS 5.04 and no claim of a further delay gain. The expected gain here is area (-2048 flip-flops, roughly -70 clusters) and the slack restored on the baseline's worst stage, at an unchanged block and tile count.

Candidates left, and why.

  • activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 mult_add, one product each): left. Each is one product plus an addend that comes from outside the product, and the block has no port for an addend — the add would stay in soft logic anyway. Worse, the block's out is an unconditional running sum: to make it deliver a single product it would have to be cleared on every cycle, and a reset sampled high at every edge holds every one of its registers at 0. There is no correct mapping, so no T1 or T2 conversion exists. They also sit on activation's own reset, so they could not share tiles with the 256 PE blocks and would add tiles for nothing.
  • norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (4 sop products whose sum sits in top): left. Their sum is a spatial chain of four products added in one cycle; the block sums its own products across cycles, one per edge. Carrying that sum in one block would need the four products serialized over four cycles, which changes the per-cycle value at the module port that top reads. Converting them as four separate products runs into the same "no bare product" problem as the rows below, and they are on a different reset from the PE blocks, so they would add tiles.
  • norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 multiply, no sum): left. A bare product cannot be read out of an accumulating block; recovering it would need out(k) - out(k-1), i.e. an 18-bit subtractor plus an 18-bit register per product in soft logic — more soft logic than the hard multiply it replaces, plus tiles on norm's reset that cannot share with the PE blocks.
  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (256 instances): converted, as described above. It is the only region whose arithmetic is literally acc += a*b with the accumulator read nowhere but through one down-cast, and it is the only one large enough for the tile cost to pay for itself.

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.os.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 56 of the 88 tiles (the 256 element products among them; the split by family is not on file).

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds. Registers the rewrite adds have no initial value to rely on either.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The rewrite is verified under that assumption.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. tpu_like.small.os.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan whose header is below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (one region; each block holds its own running sum — the out_temp accumulator of its own seq_mac instance, line 4700)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 116
STAGE_NS: 5.04
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (one per PE, 256 total, module at lines 4666-4718): a <- {1'b0, a} (the seq_mac port a = the PE's in_a, 8 bits zero-extended to 9); b <- {1'b0, b} (the seq_mac port b = the PE's in_b[DWIDTH-1:0], lines 4455/4551/4648); clk <- clk; reset <- the seq_mac port reset, which every PE drives with effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110, instances 4112-4369); out[15:0] -> the saturating down-cast logic of lines 4704-4716 (it replaces out_temp as that logic's input); out[17:16] unused. The down-cast result is then registered into the module's 8-bit output out (new 8 flip-flops per PE), which replaces the 16-bit out_temp` register of the kept candidate.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, line 4110) is asserted at least once before the first operands are applied, and is asserted only at points where the PE accumulators are meant to restart from zero. Support: the original out_temp (lines 4699-4701) has no reset path at all, so the block's mandatory synchronous clear is the only way to reach the "every register of the original starts at 0" state required for a power-up-unknown block; pe_reset exists exactly to clear the PEs — it is a self-clearing config bit, written by software at REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR bit 15 (line 4883) and cleared again in IDLE (line 4868), itself reset to 0 at line 4852. This is the same assumption the kept candidate was built and measured under; the rewrite below is cycle-for-cycle identical to the kept candidate, so it is verified under exactly the same condition.

PLAN:

What changes versus the kept candidate. The kept candidate is right about which region to convert; its one weakness is where it puts the register that restores the third register stage. It keeps a 16-bit soft register (out_temp, 16 flip-flops x 256 PEs = 4096 flip-flops) between the block's out and the down-cast, so the design still contains the full baseline stage out_temp -> down-cast (3 LUT levels) -> output_logic mux -> c_data_out — the stage the baseline reports as its slowest (5.98 ns, "a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT"; the 2.64 ns wire is the haul from a PE in the array to output_logic). This revision keeps everything else the same and moves that register to the far side of the down-cast: the down-cast is computed combinationally from the block's out, and only its 8-bit result is registered. Same values on every cycle, 8 flip-flops per PE instead of 16 (-2048 flip-flops overall), and the baseline's longest stage is cut into two roughly equal halves.

The edit (all of it inside module seq_mac, lines 4666-4718; no other module changes). Replace the body of seq_mac with:

  • output reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out; (the port becomes a register; it was a wire driven by the assign` at 4704).
  • Delete out_temp, mul_out, add_out, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp, mul_out_temp_reg, the two always blocks at 4683-4686 and 4691-4693, the qmult instance (4689), the qadd instance (4697), the always block at 4699-4701, and the assign out = ... at 4704-4716.
  • Instantiate mac_int_9x9 u_mac_hard(.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(mac_out)); with wire [17:0] mac_out;.
  • Add wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] sum_next = mac_out[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; — the low 16 bits of the block's running sum are exactly what the old 16-bit qadd accumulator held (both are the running sum taken modulo 2^16; the block wraps at 18 bits, so its low 16 bits wrap at 2^16 as well). Bits mac_out[17:16] are dropped, as the original truncated the same way.
  • Add a combinational wire [DWIDTH-1:0] downcast_next that is the expression of lines 4704-4716 verbatim with out_temp replaced by sum_next (sign bit sum_next[15] passed through; |sum_next[14:7] as the overflow test; saturate to all-ones / all-zeros or pass sum_next[6:0]`).
  • Add always @(posedge clk) begin if (reset) out <= 0; else out <= downcast_next; end.

out is the only thing the PE exposes from the mac (assign out_c = out_mac; at 4453/4549/4646), so nothing else reads out_temp or the product; no value moved inside a block is needed anywhere else, and no recomputation outside is required.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same. Write a(k), b(k) for the port values sampled at edge k and D() for the down-cast function. Original (lines 4683-4701): out_temp(n) = sum over j <= n-2 of a(j)b(j) mod 2^16, and out_c(n) = D(out_temp(n)). Block: operands sampled at edge k are added into out at edge k+1, so mac_out(n) = sum over j <= n-1 of a(j)b(j) mod 2^18; hence mac_out(n-1)[15:0] = out_temp(n). The new register gives out(n) = D(mac_out(n-1)[15:0]) = D(out_temp(n)) = out_c(n). At a reset edge the register is forced to 0, and D(0) = {0, 7'b0} = 0, so the reset branch agrees with the cleared accumulator; the block's own clear flushes its operand registers and its sum at the same edge, so after a reset at edge k both the kept candidate and this version produce the first non-zero result at edge k+3 from the operands sampled at edge k+1. This version is therefore identical, cycle by cycle, to the kept candidate that was already built and measured, and identical to the unmodified design under the ASSUMES line above.

Register stages, before and after (converted region). Before: in_a/in_b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2) -> qadd with out_temp -> out_temp (stage 3) -> combinational down-cast -> out_c. After: in_a/in_b -> block operand register (stage 1) -> multiply -> block out running-sum register (stage 2) -> combinational down-cast -> new 8-bit out register (stage 3) -> out_c. Three register stages before and after, so out_c carries the same value on the same cycle; no latency changes anywhere in the systolic array, and out_a/out_b (lines 4650-4659 etc.) are untouched.

Blocks and tiles. 256 blocks, one per seq_mac, all on the one net effective_rst and the one clock clk, so any four may share a tile. The block set, their port connections and their reset net are identical to the kept candidate, which packed into 116 DSP tiles at seed 1 (64 would be the four-to-a-tile ideal; the packer did not reach it, and nothing in this revision changes the block set, so 116 is the honest number to state rather than the ideal). The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in activation, 16 in norm) stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode and are included in that 116. Against the baseline's 88 tiles this is +28 tiles (+7.1e6 area units); it is paid for by the soft logic removed below and by the 16% shorter period and 14% shorter wirelength already measured.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block's output reaches another block: every out goes through soft logic into a register. The single-block stage is operand register -> multiply -> out register = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the slowest stage.

What soft logic leaves which stage, and which stage is slowest. Per PE, leaving the design: a_flopped + b_flopped (16 flip-flops) and mul_out_temp_reg (16 flip-flops) become the block's two internal register ranks; the 16-bit qadd (16 adder cells) becomes the block's running sum; out_temp (16 flip-flops) is replaced by an 8-bit register. Net per PE against the baseline: -40 flip-flops and -16 soft adder cells; across 256 PEs, -10240 flip-flops and -4096 adder cells. Against the kept candidate specifically, -2048 flip-flops. The stages affected:

  • The accumulate stage mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp disappears entirely (it was not the slowest).
  • The baseline's slowest stage, out_temp -> 3 down-cast LUT levels -> output_logic mux -> c_data_out (5.98 ns in the baseline, with a 2.64 ns wire into the last LUT), is split in two by the new register: (i) block out -> down-cast -> new register = 0.060 + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic) + ~2 x 0.861 (soft levels) + 0.153 + 0.019 ~ 2.9 ns, and (ii) new register -> 2.64 ns wire -> output_logic LUT -> c_data_out flip-flop ~ 2.9 ns. Both are far below the period, and this also removes the risk that would come from driving the long PE-to-output_logic haul straight out of a DSP output.
  • The slowest stage left is one this rewrite does not touch: the a-data soft path measured at seed 1 on the kept candidate, a register through four LUTs (new_n56609_1, new_n56607, new_n56604_1, $and~1165^Y~4) into the block's a input register, 5.037 ns, of which 2.85 ns is wire inside the LUT chain and 1.417 ns is the wire into the block. It ends at a block input register (setup 0.019), it contains no block and no arithmetic of any candidate region, and it bounds the period from below at this seed — hence STAGE_NS 5.04 and no claim of a further delay gain. The expected gain here is area (-2048 flip-flops, roughly -70 clusters) and the slack restored on the baseline's worst stage, at an unchanged block and tile count.

Candidates left, and why.

  • activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 mult_add, one product each): left. Each is one product plus an addend that comes from outside the product, and the block has no port for an addend — the add would stay in soft logic anyway. Worse, the block's out is an unconditional running sum: to make it deliver a single product it would have to be cleared on every cycle, and a reset sampled high at every edge holds every one of its registers at 0. There is no correct mapping, so no T1 or T2 conversion exists. They also sit on activation's own reset, so they could not share tiles with the 256 PE blocks and would add tiles for nothing.
  • norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (4 sop products whose sum sits in top): left. Their sum is a spatial chain of four products added in one cycle; the block sums its own products across cycles, one per edge. Carrying that sum in one block would need the four products serialized over four cycles, which changes the per-cycle value at the module port that top reads. Converting them as four separate products runs into the same "no bare product" problem as the rows below, and they are on a different reset from the PE blocks, so they would add tiles.
  • norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 multiply, no sum): left. A bare product cannot be read out of an accumulating block; recovering it would need out(k) - out(k-1), i.e. an 18-bit subtractor plus an 18-bit register per product in soft logic — more soft logic than the hard multiply it replaces, plus tiles on norm's reset that cannot share with the PE blocks.
  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (256 instances): converted, as described above. It is the only region whose arithmetic is literally acc += a*b with the accumulator read nowhere but through one down-cast, and it is the only one large enough for the tile cost to pay for itself.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.v

response

I'll read the design source now.

APPROVE

Verification notes:

  • Mapping/ports. seq_mac (lines 4666-4718) has DWIDTH=8 (line 74); a/b are 8-bit and zero-extended to the block's 9-bit unsigned ports, matching qmult's unsigned * at line 4725. The reset port (4669) is indeed unused in the current body and is driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110) through all three PE flavors (seq_mac instances at 4455, 4551, 4648). All cited lines check out, including 4852/4868/4883 for pe_reset.
  • Visibility. out_temp, mul_out_temp_reg, a_flopped, b_flopped are read only inside seq_mac (grep: 4673-4715 only); the PE exposes only out_c = out_mac (4453/4549/4646). Nothing moved inside the block is read elsewhere, so no recomputation is owed.
  • Cycle equivalence. Original out_temp(n) = Σ_{j≤n-2} a(j)b(j) mod 2^16; block out(n) = Σ_{j≤n-1} a(j)b(j) mod 2^18, so mac_out(n-1)[15:0] = out_temp(n). Registering the down-cast of mac_out therefore reproduces out_c(n) = D(out_temp(n)) on the same cycle — 3 register stages before and after, as stated. D(0) = 0 (positive branch, |16'b0 == 0 → {0, 7'b0}), so the forced-0 reset branch agrees with the kept candidate's out_temp <= 0 + combinational D. The two versions are bit-identical every cycle.
  • Truncation. out_temp is a 16-bit mod-2^16 accumulator (qadd, 4730-4737); the low 16 bits of a mod-2^18 sum are the same bits, so dropping mac_out[17:16] is sound.
  • ASSUMES. Correctly required: out_temp (4699-4701) has no reset path, while the block's clear is mandatory, so reset-only-when-accumulators-restart is the necessary and only available condition; cited lines support it.
  • Left regions. All 33 rows are accounted for. The activation mult_add addend at 5482 comes from data_intercept_delayed, outside the product — no addend port exists; the norm products at 5067 are visible outside via out_data_internal = variance_applied_data (5104), so they cannot be hidden inside an accumulating block. Both reasons hold against the source.
  • Tile count, STAGE_NS and the delay breakdowns are cost claims left to measurement.

prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.os.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int8). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds. Registers the rewrite adds have no initial value to rely on either.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The rewrite is verified under that assumption.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (one region; each block holds its own running sum — the out_temp accumulator of its own seq_mac instance, line 4700)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 116
STAGE_NS: 5.04
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (one per PE, 256 total, module at lines 4666-4718): a <- {1'b0, a} (the seq_mac port a = the PE's in_a, 8 bits zero-extended to 9); b <- {1'b0, b} (the seq_mac port b = the PE's in_b[DWIDTH-1:0], lines 4455/4551/4648); clk <- clk; reset <- the seq_mac port reset, which every PE drives with effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110, instances 4112-4369); out[15:0] -> the saturating down-cast logic of lines 4704-4716 (it replaces out_temp as that logic's input); out[17:16] unused. The down-cast result is then registered into the module's 8-bit output out (new 8 flip-flops per PE), which replaces the 16-bit out_temp` register of the kept candidate.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, line 4110) is asserted at least once before the first operands are applied, and is asserted only at points where the PE accumulators are meant to restart from zero. Support: the original out_temp (lines 4699-4701) has no reset path at all, so the block's mandatory synchronous clear is the only way to reach the "every register of the original starts at 0" state required for a power-up-unknown block; pe_reset exists exactly to clear the PEs — it is a self-clearing config bit, written by software at REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR bit 15 (line 4883) and cleared again in IDLE (line 4868), itself reset to 0 at line 4852. This is the same assumption the kept candidate was built and measured under; the rewrite below is cycle-for-cycle identical to the kept candidate, so it is verified under exactly the same condition.

PLAN:

What changes versus the kept candidate. The kept candidate is right about which region to convert; its one weakness is where it puts the register that restores the third register stage. It keeps a 16-bit soft register (out_temp, 16 flip-flops x 256 PEs = 4096 flip-flops) between the block's out and the down-cast, so the design still contains the full baseline stage out_temp -> down-cast (3 LUT levels) -> output_logic mux -> c_data_out — the stage the baseline reports as its slowest (5.98 ns, "a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT"; the 2.64 ns wire is the haul from a PE in the array to output_logic). This revision keeps everything else the same and moves that register to the far side of the down-cast: the down-cast is computed combinationally from the block's out, and only its 8-bit result is registered. Same values on every cycle, 8 flip-flops per PE instead of 16 (-2048 flip-flops overall), and the baseline's longest stage is cut into two roughly equal halves.

The edit (all of it inside module seq_mac, lines 4666-4718; no other module changes). Replace the body of seq_mac with:

  • output reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out; (the port becomes a register; it was a wire driven by the assign` at 4704).
  • Delete out_temp, mul_out, add_out, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp, mul_out_temp_reg, the two always blocks at 4683-4686 and 4691-4693, the qmult instance (4689), the qadd instance (4697), the always block at 4699-4701, and the assign out = ... at 4704-4716.
  • Instantiate mac_int_9x9 u_mac_hard(.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(mac_out)); with wire [17:0] mac_out;.
  • Add wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] sum_next = mac_out[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; — the low 16 bits of the block's running sum are exactly what the old 16-bit qadd accumulator held (both are the running sum taken modulo 2^16; the block wraps at 18 bits, so its low 16 bits wrap at 2^16 as well). Bits mac_out[17:16] are dropped, as the original truncated the same way.
  • Add a combinational wire [DWIDTH-1:0] downcast_next that is the expression of lines 4704-4716 verbatim with out_temp replaced by sum_next (sign bit sum_next[15] passed through; |sum_next[14:7] as the overflow test; saturate to all-ones / all-zeros or pass sum_next[6:0]`).
  • Add always @(posedge clk) begin if (reset) out <= 0; else out <= downcast_next; end.

out is the only thing the PE exposes from the mac (assign out_c = out_mac; at 4453/4549/4646), so nothing else reads out_temp or the product; no value moved inside a block is needed anywhere else, and no recomputation outside is required.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same. Write a(k), b(k) for the port values sampled at edge k and D() for the down-cast function. Original (lines 4683-4701): out_temp(n) = sum over j <= n-2 of a(j)b(j) mod 2^16, and out_c(n) = D(out_temp(n)). Block: operands sampled at edge k are added into out at edge k+1, so mac_out(n) = sum over j <= n-1 of a(j)b(j) mod 2^18; hence mac_out(n-1)[15:0] = out_temp(n). The new register gives out(n) = D(mac_out(n-1)[15:0]) = D(out_temp(n)) = out_c(n). At a reset edge the register is forced to 0, and D(0) = {0, 7'b0} = 0, so the reset branch agrees with the cleared accumulator; the block's own clear flushes its operand registers and its sum at the same edge, so after a reset at edge k both the kept candidate and this version produce the first non-zero result at edge k+3 from the operands sampled at edge k+1. This version is therefore identical, cycle by cycle, to the kept candidate that was already built and measured, and identical to the unmodified design under the ASSUMES line above.

Register stages, before and after (converted region). Before: in_a/in_b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2) -> qadd with out_temp -> out_temp (stage 3) -> combinational down-cast -> out_c. After: in_a/in_b -> block operand register (stage 1) -> multiply -> block out running-sum register (stage 2) -> combinational down-cast -> new 8-bit out register (stage 3) -> out_c. Three register stages before and after, so out_c carries the same value on the same cycle; no latency changes anywhere in the systolic array, and out_a/out_b (lines 4650-4659 etc.) are untouched.

Blocks and tiles. 256 blocks, one per seq_mac, all on the one net effective_rst and the one clock clk, so any four may share a tile. The block set, their port connections and their reset net are identical to the kept candidate, which packed into 116 DSP tiles at seed 1 (64 would be the four-to-a-tile ideal; the packer did not reach it, and nothing in this revision changes the block set, so 116 is the honest number to state rather than the ideal). The 32 remaining multiplies (16 in activation, 16 in norm) stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode and are included in that 116. Against the baseline's 88 tiles this is +28 tiles (+7.1e6 area units); it is paid for by the soft logic removed below and by the 16% shorter period and 14% shorter wirelength already measured.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block's output reaches another block: every out goes through soft logic into a register. The single-block stage is operand register -> multiply -> out register = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the slowest stage.

What soft logic leaves which stage, and which stage is slowest. Per PE, leaving the design: a_flopped + b_flopped (16 flip-flops) and mul_out_temp_reg (16 flip-flops) become the block's two internal register ranks; the 16-bit qadd (16 adder cells) becomes the block's running sum; out_temp (16 flip-flops) is replaced by an 8-bit register. Net per PE against the baseline: -40 flip-flops and -16 soft adder cells; across 256 PEs, -10240 flip-flops and -4096 adder cells. Against the kept candidate specifically, -2048 flip-flops. The stages affected:

  • The accumulate stage mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp disappears entirely (it was not the slowest).
  • The baseline's slowest stage, out_temp -> 3 down-cast LUT levels -> output_logic mux -> c_data_out (5.98 ns in the baseline, with a 2.64 ns wire into the last LUT), is split in two by the new register: (i) block out -> down-cast -> new register = 0.060 + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic) + ~2 x 0.861 (soft levels) + 0.153 + 0.019 ~ 2.9 ns, and (ii) new register -> 2.64 ns wire -> output_logic LUT -> c_data_out flip-flop ~ 2.9 ns. Both are far below the period, and this also removes the risk that would come from driving the long PE-to-output_logic haul straight out of a DSP output.
  • The slowest stage left is one this rewrite does not touch: the a-data soft path measured at seed 1 on the kept candidate, a register through four LUTs (new_n56609_1, new_n56607, new_n56604_1, $and~1165^Y~4) into the block's a input register, 5.037 ns, of which 2.85 ns is wire inside the LUT chain and 1.417 ns is the wire into the block. It ends at a block input register (setup 0.019), it contains no block and no arithmetic of any candidate region, and it bounds the period from below at this seed — hence STAGE_NS 5.04 and no claim of a further delay gain. The expected gain here is area (-2048 flip-flops, roughly -70 clusters) and the slack restored on the baseline's worst stage, at an unchanged block and tile count.

Candidates left, and why.

  • activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 mult_add, one product each): left. Each is one product plus an addend that comes from outside the product, and the block has no port for an addend — the add would stay in soft logic anyway. Worse, the block's out is an unconditional running sum: to make it deliver a single product it would have to be cleared on every cycle, and a reset sampled high at every edge holds every one of its registers at 0. There is no correct mapping, so no T1 or T2 conversion exists. They also sit on activation's own reset, so they could not share tiles with the 256 PE blocks and would add tiles for nothing.
  • norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (4 sop products whose sum sits in top): left. Their sum is a spatial chain of four products added in one cycle; the block sums its own products across cycles, one per edge. Carrying that sum in one block would need the four products serialized over four cycles, which changes the per-cycle value at the module port that top reads. Converting them as four separate products runs into the same "no bare product" problem as the rows below, and they are on a different reset from the PE blocks, so they would add tiles.
  • norm.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 multiply, no sum): left. A bare product cannot be read out of an accumulating block; recovering it would need out(k) - out(k-1), i.e. an 18-bit subtractor plus an 18-bit register per product in soft logic — more soft logic than the hard multiply it replaces, plus tiles on norm's reset that cannot share with the PE blocks.
  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (256 instances): converted, as described above. It is the only region whose arithmetic is literally acc += a*b with the accumulator read nowhere but through one down-cast, and it is the only one large enough for the tile cost to pay for itself.
response

I'll start by reading the design region and the check script.

check.sh passes and the census shows the 256 block instances the plan calls for.

What changed

Region qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) — module seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666), the one module edited; all 256 PE instances follow from it.

Removed from the module body: out_temp, mul_out, add_out, a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_temp, mul_out_temp_reg; the operand-flop always block; the qmult mult_u1 instance; the product-register always block; the qadd add_u1 instance; the accumulator always block; and the assign out = ... down-cast.

Added in their place:

  • mac_int_9x9 u_mac_hard(.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(mac_out)); with wire [17:0] mac_out; — every input port connected by name, the 8-bit operands zero-extended to 9 bits (the original qmult multiplied them unsigned), reset driven by the module's own reset port, which each PE drives with effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110).
  • wire [15:0] sum_next = mac_out[15:0]; — the low 16 bits of the block's running sum, equal to the old 16-bit qadd accumulator value (both wrap, 2^18 vs 2^16, and bits 17:16 are dropped exactly as the original truncated).
  • wire [7:0] downcast_next = ... — the down-cast expression of the old lines 4704–4716 verbatim with out_temp replaced by sum_next.
  • The port changed to output reg [7:0] out; driven by always @(posedge clk) if (reset) out <= 0; else out <= downcast_next; — the third register stage moved to the far side of the down-cast (8 flip-flops per PE instead of the 16-bit out_temp).

Register stages are three before and after (in_a/in_b → block operand register → multiply → block running-sum register → down-cast → new out register), so out_c carries the same value on the same cycle; out_a/out_b paths are untouched. qmult and qadd module definitions are left in the file and are now uninstantiated; yosys drops them as unused and the top module is still top.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, line 4110, driven to every PE at lines 4112–4369) is asserted at least once before the first operands are applied, and is asserted only where the PE accumulators are meant to restart from zero. Support: the original out_temp (lines 4699–4701, pre-edit) had no reset path, so the block's mandatory synchronous clear is the only way to reach the "every register of the original starts at 0" state for a power-up-unknown block; pe_reset is the self-clearing config bit written at REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR bit 15 (line 4883), cleared in IDLE (line 4868) and reset to 0 at line 4852.

Census: mac_int_9x9 256; soft $mul 288 → 32 and $add 288 → 32 (the 16 activation and 16 norm multiplies left unconverted, as planned).

12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds. Registers the rewrite adds have no initial value to rely on either.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The rewrite is verified under that assumption.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
